This work is supported by the National Science Foundation of China under Grants No. 12347105, No. 12375099 and No. 12047503, and the National Key Research and Development Program of China Grant No. 2020YFC2201501, No. 2021YFA0718304.

Appendix A
Spherical and tensor basis

The spherical basis used to convert between spherical components and Cartesian tensor components is specified. The vector spherical basis is

\displaystyle e^{i}_{+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1,i,0),\qquad e^{i}_{0}=(0,0,1),\qquad e^{i}_{-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1,-i,0),

(A.1)

where
i
denotes the Cartesian components
(x,y,z)
. The dual basis is defined by

\displaystyle\bar{e}^{i}_{m}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(e^{i}_{m})^{*}=(-1)^{m}e^{i}_{-m}.

(A.2)

For any Cartesian vector
V^{i}
, the spherical and Cartesian components are related by

\displaystyle V_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{i}_{m}V^{i},\qquad V^{i}=\sum_{m=-1}^{1}\bar{e}^{i}_{m}V_{m}.

(A.3)

Higher-rank spherical basis tensors are obtained by coupling several vector spherical bases with CGCs,

\displaystyle e^{i_{1}\cdots i_{L}}_{LM}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{L}}C^{LM}_{1m_{1};\cdots;1m_{L}}e^{i_{1}}_{m_{1}}\cdots e^{i_{L}}_{m_{L}},

(A.4)

where
C^{LM}_{1m_{1};\cdots;1m_{L}}
denotes the successive CGCs projection of
L
spin-one indices onto the irreducible rank-
L
representation. The first few cases are

\displaystyle e_{00}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1,\qquad e^{i}_{1m}=e^{i}_{m},\qquad e^{ij}_{2M}=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}}C^{2,M}_{1,m_{1};1,m_{2}}e^{i}_{m_{1}}e^{j}_{m_{2}}.

(A.5)

The corresponding dual tensors used for Cartesian reconstruction are

\displaystyle\bar{e}^{i_{1}\cdots i_{L}}_{LM}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(e^{i_{1}\cdots i_{L}}_{LM}\right)^{*},\qquad\bar{e}^{ij}_{2M}=\left(e^{ij}_{2M}\right)^{*}.

(A.6)

Both
e^{ij}_{2M}
and
\bar{e}^{ij}_{2M}
are symmetric and traceless in their Cartesian indices. The tensor
e^{ij}_{2M}
projects a Cartesian tensor onto its spherical component, while
\bar{e}^{ij}_{2M}
reconstructs the Cartesian tensor from its spherical components.

Appendix B
Clebsch-Gordan coefficients

B.1
CGCs calculation

Consider two canonical states
\left|s_{1}\sigma_{1}\right\rangle
and
\left|s_{2}\sigma_{2}\right\rangle
. Our goal is to couple these two states into a total spin-
S
, such that,

|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq S\leq s_{1}+s_{2}.

(B.1)

The states are related by a linear relation given by

\left|S\sigma\right\rangle=\sum_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}C^{s_{1},\sigma_{1};s_{2},\sigma_{2}}_{S,\sigma}\left|s_{1}\sigma_{1}\right\rangle\left|s_{2}\sigma_{2}\right\rangle,

(B.2)

where the coefficients
C^{s_{1},\sigma_{1};s_{2},\sigma_{2}}_{S,\sigma}
are called CGCs.
We have that
\sigma=\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}
.
An expression for the CG coefficients was also derived by Wigner
[
43
]

\displaystyle C^{s_{1},\sigma_{1};s_{2},\sigma_{2}}_{S,\sigma}=

\displaystyle\delta_{\sigma}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}

(B.3)

\displaystyle\times\left[(2S+1)\frac{(S+s_{1}-s_{2})!(S-s_{1}+s_{2})!(s_{1}+s_{2}-S)!(S+\sigma)!(S-\sigma)!}{(S+s_{1}+s_{2}+1)!(s_{1}-\sigma_{1})!(s_{1}+\sigma_{1})!(s_{2}-\sigma_{2})!(s_{2}+\sigma_{2})!}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{t}\frac{(-1)^{t+S+\sigma}}{t!}\frac{(S+\sigma_{1}+s_{2}-t)!(s_{1}-\sigma_{1}+t)!}{S-(s_{1}+s_{2}-t)!(S+\sigma-t)!(t+s_{1}-s_{2}-\sigma)!}.

The sum is taken over all values of
t
which lead to non negative factorials.
Using the orthogonality relations, we can get the inverse relations

\left|s_{1}\sigma_{1}\right\rangle\left|s_{2}\sigma_{2}\right\rangle=\sum_{S}C_{s_{1},\sigma_{1};s_{2},\sigma_{2}}^{S,\sigma}\left|S\sigma\right\rangle.

(B.4)

The CGCs have the following symmetry properties:

\displaystyle C^{s_{1},\sigma_{1};s_{2},\sigma_{2}}_{S,\sigma}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s_{1}+s_{2}-S}C^{s_{1},-\sigma_{1};s_{2},-\sigma_{2}}_{S,-\sigma},

(B.5)

\displaystyle C^{s_{1},\sigma_{1};s_{2},\sigma_{2}}_{S,\sigma}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s_{1}+s_{2}-S}C^{s_{2},\sigma_{2};s_{1},\sigma_{1}}_{S,\sigma},

(B.6)

\displaystyle C^{s_{1},\sigma_{1};s_{2},\sigma_{2}}_{S,\sigma}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s_{1}-\sigma_{1}}\left[\frac{2S+1}{2s_{2}+1}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}C^{s_{1},\sigma_{1};S,-\sigma}_{s_{2},-\sigma_{2}}.

(B.7)

B.2
Examples of
\mathrm{SU}(2)
CGCs calculation

We first present some examples of
\mathrm{SU}(2)
CGC calculations. The
\mathrm{SU}(2)
CGCs are

C_{s,\{J\}}^{s_{1},\{I\};s_{2},\{K\}}\equiv\sqrt{\dfrac{(a+b)!(b+c)!(a+c+1)!}{b!(a+b+c+1)!a!c!}}\delta_{(J_{1}\cdots J_{a}}^{(I_{1}\cdots I_{a}}\varepsilon^{I_{a+1}\cdots I_{a+b}),(K_{1}\cdots K_{b}}\delta_{J_{a+1}\cdots J_{a+c})}^{K_{b+1}\cdots K_{b+c})},

(B.8)

with

\displaystyle a=

\displaystyle s_{1}+s-s_{2},

(B.9)

\displaystyle b=

\displaystyle s_{1}+s_{2}-s,

\displaystyle c=

\displaystyle s_{2}+s-s_{1},

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{I_{1}\cdots I_{b},K_{1}\cdots K_{b}}=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{1}}\cdots\varepsilon^{I_{b}K_{b}}.

1.

s=\frac{1}{2},s_{1}=\frac{1}{2},s_{2}=0
with
a=1,b=0,c=0
. The CGC is

C_{\frac{1}{2},\{J_{1}\}}^{\frac{1}{2},\{I_{1}\};0}=\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}.

(B.10)

2.

s=1,s_{1}=1,s_{2}=0
with
a=2,b=0,c=0
. The CGC is

\displaystyle C_{1,\{J_{1}J_{2}\}}^{1,\{I_{1}I_{2}\};0}=

\displaystyle\delta_{(J_{1}J_{2})}^{(I_{1}I_{2})}

(B.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{2}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{1}}).

3.

s=\frac{3}{2},s_{1}=\frac{3}{2},s_{2}=0
with
a=3,b=0,c=0
. The CGC is

\displaystyle C_{\frac{3}{2},\{J_{1}J_{2}J_{3}\}}^{\frac{3}{2},\{I_{1}I_{2}I_{3}\};0}=

\displaystyle\delta_{(J_{1}J_{2}J_{3})}^{(I_{1}I_{2}I_{3})}

(B.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}(\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{2}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{I_{3}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{3}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{I_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{2}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{I_{3}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{2}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{3}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{I_{1}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{3}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{I_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{3}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{2}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{I_{1}}).

4.

s=\frac{1}{2},s_{1}=\frac{1}{2},s_{2}=1
with
a=0,b=1,c=1
. The CGC is

\displaystyle C_{\frac{1}{2},\{J_{1}\}}^{\frac{1}{2},\{I_{1}\};1,\{K_{1}K_{2}\}}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}(K_{1}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{2})}

(B.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{1}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{2}}+\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{2}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{1}}\right).

5.

s=\frac{3}{2},s_{1}=\frac{1}{2},s_{2}=1
with
a=1,b=0,c=2
. The CGC is

\displaystyle C_{\frac{3}{2},\{J_{1}J_{2}J_{3}\}}^{\frac{1}{2},\{I_{1}\};1,\{K_{1}K_{2}\}}=

\displaystyle\delta_{(J_{1}J_{2}J_{3})}^{I_{1}(K_{1}K_{2})}

(B.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}(\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{K_{1}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{K_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{1}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{K_{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{3}}^{K_{1}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{3}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{3}}^{I_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{K_{1}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{2}}).

6.

s=\frac{1}{2},s_{1}=\frac{3}{2},s_{2}=1
with
a=1,b=2,c=0
. The CGC is

\displaystyle C_{\frac{1}{2},\{J_{1}\}}^{\frac{3}{2},\{I_{1}I_{2}I_{3}\};1,\{K_{1}K_{2}\}}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{(I_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}I_{3}),(K_{1}K_{2})}

(B.15)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}(\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}K_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{3}K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{3}K_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{2}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{3}K_{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{2}}\varepsilon^{I_{3}K_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{3}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{3}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}K_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{2}}.

7.

s=1,s_{1}=1,s_{2}=1
with
a=1,b=1,c=1
. The CGC is

\displaystyle C_{1,\{J_{1}J_{2}\}}^{1,\{I_{1}I_{2}\};1,\{K_{1}K_{2}\}}=

\displaystyle\delta_{(J_{1}}^{(I_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2})(K_{1}}\delta_{J_{2})}^{K_{2})}

(B.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}(\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}K_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}K_{2}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{K_{1}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{2}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{1}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{1}}^{I_{2}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{2}}\delta_{J_{2}}^{K_{1}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}K_{1}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{1}}\varepsilon^{I_{2}K_{2}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{1}}+\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{2}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{1}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{2}}+\delta_{J_{2}}^{I_{2}}\varepsilon^{I_{1}K_{2}}\delta_{J_{1}}^{K_{1}}).
